# Numerical package GAMS review

All 174 selected packages with a top-level A–M or P assignment have been reviewed at their pinned revisions: 173 have procedure mappings and 1 supplies supporting declarations rather than numerical procedures. There are 454 procedure records. This scope includes arithmetic, statistics, geometry, and supporting data utilities, alongside numerical solvers.

This is an initial procedure-level coverage pass, not an exhaustive inventory of every routine or every overload. Specific assignments require evidence for the named procedure and configuration. Other package capabilities remain discovery classifications. Source review does not imply a new compilation test, accuracy certification, or benchmark.

The [Netlib GAMS taxonomy](https://netlib.org/misc/gams.html) supplies the additional detailed labels and hierarchy; the existing top-level and immediate classifications come from [NIST GAMS](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi). Labels such as “General” must be read with their ancestors. C1/C10 and L1/L10 are siblings, and explicit parent links preserve that distinction.

## Coverage and remaining work

“Discovery only” lists existing immediate package assignments without a matching procedure record. These are a review queue, not evidence that a capability is missing. No class is forced onto a package merely to fill a gap.

## Automatic differentiation

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| differentia: `differentia` / `derivative / gradient / jacobian` | [H1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/H1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/Nicholaswogan/Differentia/blob/2388e8ecf300a7bbc0904feab47023413464edf9/src/differentia_derivative.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/Nicholaswogan/Differentia/blob/2388e8ecf300a7bbc0904feab47023413464edf9/test/test_differentia.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/differentia.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-qr-autodiff.md) | Forward-mode automatic differentiation with dual numbers. The main checks the upstream scalar example against its analytical value and derivative, a two-variable polynomial gradient and a square 2-by-2 Jacobian against exact formulas. GNU heap trampolines support the internal callback procedures in the restricted sandbox. Library sources are unchanged. The author limits jacobian to square problems and documents incomplete intrinsic coverage; sparse interfaces, every overload and all failure modes are not validated. |

## Computational geometry

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| kdtree2: `kdtree2_module` / `kdtree2_create / kdtree2_n_nearest / kdtree2_r_nearest` | [P](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/P), [N5](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/N5) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jmhodges/kdtree2/blob/dc757acec3162948cb228c2e0457d10134ec1edc/src-f90/kdtree2.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jmhodges/kdtree2/blob/dc757acec3162948cb228c2e0457d10134ec1edc/src-f90/kdtree2_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/kdtree2.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-spatial-nufft.md) | Nearest-neighbor and radius searches. An FPM wrapper selects unchanged src-f90/kdtree2.f90; the archived upstream single-precision setting is retained. The checked main builds a tree for 30 deterministic 2D points, compares four nearest indices and squared distances against an independent direct search, and checks radius-search count, membership, distances and uniqueness. The original Academic Free License 1.1 is retained. No source edits. The implementation has global search state and is not offered as thread-safe; the old interface, C++ code, every dimension and large-scale performance are not validated. The class-specific N5 driver additionally verifies 36 3D searches over 48 points against exhaustive real64 distances: k=1,5,48, radii squared 0/0.125/4 and both storage rearrangement modes. Tie ordering is not prescribed; complete radius sets and uniqueness are checked. This driver does not separately verify the broad P mapping. |
| raffle: `raffle__dist_calcs` / `get_min_dist / get_min_dist_between_point_and_atom` | [P](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/P) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/ExeQuantCode/RAFFLE/blob/c152a30a1eaf94965c047bcd1a7edca07873b56f/src/fortran/lib/mod_dist_calcs.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/ExeQuantCode/RAFFLE/blob/c152a30a1eaf94965c047bcd1a7edca07873b56f/test/test_dist_calcs.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/raffle.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-periodic-calculus.md) | RAFFLE is pinned to release v1.0.0 because current main and v1.1.1 exceed the unchanged 16 MiB archive limit. The native FPM library is unchanged. The checked main adapts test/test_dist_calcs.f90: a two-atom silicon primitive cell and three equivalent fractional points exercise get_min_dist_between_point_and_atom and get_min_dist. Both distances must be finite and agree with sqrt(3)*0.428 within 3e-6 in REAL32. P denotes the demonstrated periodic geometry; no optimization, structure-prediction accuracy, trained model, or full upstream test-suite validation is claimed. GPL-3.0 notices are retained. The checked GAMS driver covers orthogonal cells only. A separate skew-cell probe returns 0.92970967292785645 where an independent periodic-image search gives 0.31679647220910667; source, hash and complete result are retained in docs/gams-candidates/raffle-skew. No library patch is made. |

## Differential equations

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| daskr: `external` / `ddaskr` | [I1a2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1a2) | optional user Jacobian/preconditioner callbacks | [Source](https://github.com/davidpfister/daskr/blob/b43533edc5d6900088a846d802e3a86d0f9a06ad/src/ddaskr.f#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/davidpfister/daskr/blob/b43533edc5d6900088a846d802e3a86d0f9a06ad/examples/web/dweb.f) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/daskr.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-dae-fft.md) | Differential-algebraic initial-value integration with Newton/Krylov support. |
| dde_solver: `DDE_SOLVER_M` / `DDE_SOLVER` | [I](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I) | problem-specific source or delayed derivative callback | [Source](https://github.com/WarrenWeckesser/dde_solver/blob/90d3389ce5d5733ab2b6e7ed7d2dad273b4d0c6b/dde_solver_m.f90#L376) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/WarrenWeckesser/dde_solver/blob/90d3389ce5d5733ab2b6e7ed7d2dad273b4d0c6b/tests/delay_oscillator/delay_oscillator.f90) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/WarrenWeckesser/dde_solver/blob/90d3389ce5d5733ab2b6e7ed7d2dad273b4d0c6b/tests/delay_oscillator/delay_oscillator_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/dde_solver.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-delay-embedding.md) | Delay differential equations: retain the broad differential-equation class rather than assume the ODE subclasses describe delayed problems. Dedicated I consumer checks twelve scalar constant-delay equations against independently specified method-of-steps polynomials through four delay intervals, and three oscillators against exact sine solutions. Verifies solver status and 975 finite dense-output samples including delay knots, with signed history scaling. Module callbacks use no copied upstream code. State-dependent/neutral/multiple delays, events, derivative interpolation, stiffness and all error paths are not verified. |
| ddeabm: `ddeabm_module` / `ddeabm_class%integrate` | [I1a1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1a1b) | right-hand side only | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/ddeabm/blob/dba70c8c65388638662df2694339c5ba7feaf12a/src/ddeabm_module.F90#L60) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/ddeabm/blob/dba70c8c65388638662df2694339c5ba7feaf12a/test/ddeabm_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/ddeabm.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-ode.md) | Adams-Bashforth-Moulton multistep initial-value integration for nonstiff problems. |
| dftatom: `dftatom / reigen` / `solve_radial_eigenproblem` | [I1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/certik/dftatom/blob/e49b3042de6e2471afe5eea992c9ba50dac08f95/src/reigen.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/certik/dftatom/blob/e49b3042de6e2471afe5eea992c9ba50dac08f95/src/rschroed.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/certik/dftatom/blob/e49b3042de6e2471afe5eea992c9ba50dac08f95/tests/hydrogen/hydrogen.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/dftatom.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-radial-dense.md) | Unmodified Fortran library sources selected from the upstream native makefile and wrapped for FPM. The checked example adapts the upstream hydrogen test to Z=1 and computes all six (n,l) states through n=3 on an exponential radial mesh. It requires solver convergence, finite energies and agreement with the analytical nonrelativistic energies -1/(2*n**2) within 1e-7 Hartree. Relativistic Dirac, Poisson and self-consistent density-functional calculations are present in the library but not exercised by this example. C/Python bindings and optional extra integration/interpolation/LAPACK sources are outside the selected native source set. |
| diffeq: `diffeq` / `runge_kutta_45%solve` | [I1a1a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1a1a) | right-hand side only | [Source](https://github.com/jchristopherson/diffeq/blob/989b320179c23aaff4bfe72994c90e9034f50c74/src/singlestep/explicit/diffeq_rk45.f90#L32) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jchristopherson/diffeq/blob/989b320179c23aaff4bfe72994c90e9034f50c74/examples/rk45_example.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/diffeq.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-regrid-rk45.md) | Explicit adaptive Runge-Kutta initial-value integration; implicit methods are separate types. |

The checked I1a2 driver solves the shared autonomous system y1'= -1000*y1+999*y2, y2'= -y2 from [2,1], checking exact exponential modes at four output times. Only this stiff ODE configuration is verified; no DAE capability or general stiff-problem accuracy is established. |
| fatode: `ERK_TLM_f90_Integrator` / `INTEGRATE_TLM` | [I1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1) | User-supplied analytical state Jacobian in this demo; tangent initial conditions select the rate sensitivity. | [Source](https://github.com/ComputationalScienceLaboratory/FATODE/blob/3f237c49bd377644a597c2a49ae5a83f090bfab5/TLM/ERK_TLM/ERK_TLM_f90_Integrator.F90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/ComputationalScienceLaboratory/FATODE/blob/3f237c49bd377644a597c2a49ae5a83f090bfab5/EXAMPLES/swe/SWE_ERK_TLM/swe2D_erk_tlm_dr.F90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fatode.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-tangents-blocks.md) | Selected FATODE explicit Runge-Kutta tangent-linear integrator, linked to native BLAS/LAPACK. The FPM wrapper compiles unchanged TLM/ERK_TLM/ERK_TLM_f90_Integrator.F90; bundled precompiled platform binaries are not used. The example augments exponential decay with a constant rate state and differentiates the initial rate using the tangent-linear model, checking the state, rate, and both sensitivities against analytical formulas. It explicitly selects the supplied analytical Jacobian and enables tangent-variable adaptive error control; the initial default finite-difference Jacobian settings missed the requested sensitivity accuracy. No numerical library edits. Implicit/stiff solvers, adjoint integration, sparse solver variants and all ERK methods are not offered or verified. |

Other boundary modes and positive shifts remain unverified. |

| hrweno: `hrweno_tvdode` / `rktvd%integrate` | [I1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/HugoMVale/HR-WENO/blob/2885105a7ba98351fed299b22e9bc0c67a809d2f/src/hrweno_tvdode.f90#L97) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/hrweno.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-inverse-tvd.md) | WENO spatial reconstruction and explicit TVD Runge-Kutta integration for conservation equations. The main checks a smooth periodic advection problem against exact cell averages and verifies conservation of mass; discontinuous solutions and nonuniform grids are not covered. Dedicated I1 consumer checks 12 prescribed single-step-mode integrations at orders 1..3, with 8/16/32/64 dyadic steps to t=1. Independent stability-polynomial powers, exact nonautonomous forcing, exponential solutions, order-dependent grid convergence and function-evaluation counts are required. Uses documented itask=2; ordinary interval mode has a source TODO concerning final-step overshoot and is outside this verification. Spatial WENO, multistep mode, backward integration and stiffness are not verified. |
| odepack: `odepack_mod` / `lsoda_class%integrate` | [I1a2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1a2) | optional user Jacobian; internal approximation supported | [Source](https://github.com/Nicholaswogan/odepack/blob/d5db96093dc14fff54e10575cc586fa85392b078/src/odepack_mod.f90#L13) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/Nicholaswogan/odepack/blob/d5db96093dc14fff54e10575cc586fa85392b078/test/benchmark.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/odepack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-stiff.md) | Automatic switching between Adams and BDF; classify stiff capability here without claiming all ODEPACK methods. The checked I1a2 driver solves the shared autonomous system y1'= -1000*y1+999*y2, y2'= -y2 from [2,1], checking exact exponential modes at four output times. Only this stiff ODE configuration is verified; no DAE capability or general stiff-problem accuracy is established. |
| poisfft: `PoisFFT_DP` / `PoisFFT_Solver1D / Execute / Finalize` | [I2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/LadaF/PoisFFT/blob/7e7ebcd75130553ef2cf58548ec519933a3d3426/src/poisfft-inc.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/LadaF/PoisFFT/blob/7e7ebcd75130553ef2cf58548ec519933a3d3426/src/poisfft-solvers-inc.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/LadaF/PoisFFT/blob/7e7ebcd75130553ef2cf58548ec519933a3d3426/src/testpoisson.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/poisfft.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-pde.md) | Serial FFTW Poisson solvers. An FPM wrapper selects the Fortran library modules, retains upstream template includes, adds CPP-disabled USE declarations for the FPM dependency scanner, declares unused MPI/PFFT modules external for scanning, enables CPP and links Debian FFTW 3.3.10 single/double and thread libraries. MPI/PFFT and C/C++ bindings are not offered. The checked double-precision periodic 1D spectral problem compares its solution with sin(2*pi*x) at 32 points. Other boundary conditions, finite-difference modes, nonuniform grids, higher-dimensional solvers, single precision and parallel execution are not validated. A class-specific I2 driver additionally verifies spectral and second-order finite-difference periodic solvers at 16/32/64 points against exact two-mode discrete solutions, finite-difference residuals, zero-mean gauge and reuse of each plan for zero forcing (12 solves). Finite-difference errors are separated from solver error; other boundary conditions, dimensions and parallel variants remain unverified. |

| tsunami: `mod_diff` / `diff_centered / diff_upwind` | [H1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/H1) | finite differences | [Source](https://github.com/modern-fortran/tsunami/blob/70b5702aed42bbea0733335d50e0eb70cb159e08/src/ch04/mod_diff.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/modern-fortran/tsunami/blob/70b5702aed42bbea0733335d50e0eb70cb159e08/src/ch04/tsunami.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/tsunami.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-rules-differences.md) | Only the author serial Chapter 4 mod_diff and mod_initial modules are wrapped; their contents are unchanged. The main follows the chapter shallow-water time-stepping equations on a 100-cell periodic grid for 200 steps. It checks the centered difference against a discrete sine derivative, the upwind difference against an independently indexed periodic reference, Gaussian peak/symmetry, finite water height/velocity, positive total depth, nontrivial evolution, and conservation of integrated elevation. The example uses REAL32, with REAL64 accumulation of mass. I2 classifies the demonstrated PDE calculation; H1 classifies diff_centered/diff_upwind as numerical differentiation. This is an educational finite-difference demonstration, not validation of a production tsunami model. Other chapters, coarrays and parallel scaling are not verified. MIT notices are retained. |
| twopnt: `twopnt_core` / `TwoPntBVProblem%run` | [I1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I1b) | problem residual and configured Jacobian handling | [Source](https://github.com/perazz/twopnt/blob/4ce477215f84a88d879f0ca3937cab9bedfb7762/src/twopnt_core.f90#L357) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/twopnt.f90) | Adaptive boundary-value solution; retain I1b rather than infer linearity for all supported problems. A manufactured Poisson probe u_second_derivative=-2 with zero endpoints failed the full-profile/residual checks: the pinned solver reports success but returns the preceding iterate (the zero initial guess on the linear problem). The convergence exit occurs before v0=v1 in search. Failed source/output are retained under docs/gams-candidates/twopnt; this probe is not a verified driver and no library patch has been applied. |

## Fourier transforms

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |

| 2decomp-fft: `decomp_2d_fft` / `decomp_2d_fft_3d` | [J1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/2decomp-fft/2decomp-fft/blob/4cb38be477b9457cfa5f5624c8fe09387968b190/src/fft_generic.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/2decomp-fft/2decomp-fft/blob/4cb38be477b9457cfa5f5624c8fe09387968b190/src/glassman.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/2decomp-fft/2decomp-fft/blob/4cb38be477b9457cfa5f5624c8fe09387968b190/examples/fft_physical_x/fft_c2c_x.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/2decomp-fft.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-multidimensional-fft.md) | The upstream CMake-selected CPU library uses the built-in generic Fourier transform backend, double precision and pinned MPICH. OpenMP is disabled. The example adapts the author-provided complex FFT example, initializes exactly one MPI process with self/shared-memory UCX transports, transforms an 8-by-6-by-4 complex impulse, independently checks that all forward coefficients equal one, and verifies inverse reconstruction after normalization. J1 covers the multidimensional Fourier transform. Multiple MPI processes and distributed performance are not verified. Local exports need compatible MPI development libraries and locally appropriate include/link flags. BSD-3-Clause notices are retained. |
| dtfft: `dtfft / dtfft_plan_c2c_t` / `create / execute / destroy` | [J1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/ShatrovOA/dtFFT/blob/bbfd6746be7482bd8366ccb09a73b72882573984/src/dtfft_plan.F90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/ShatrovOA/dtFFT/blob/bbfd6746be7482bd8366ccb09a73b72882573984/src/interfaces/fft/fftw/dtfft_executor_fftw_m.F90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/ShatrovOA/dtFFT/blob/bbfd6746be7482bd8366ccb09a73b72882573984/tests/fortran/test_c2c_2d_f.F90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/dtfft.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-multidimensional-fft.md) | The CPU-only Fortran target is configured upstream with FFTW enabled, MPICH, input checks, and no C/C++/Python API, GPU, compression or OpenMP support. Its complete generated configuration header and source selection are checksum-locked. The checked main initializes exactly one MPI process with self/shared-memory UCX transports, transforms an 8-by-8 complex impulse, independently checks that every forward coefficient equals one, then verifies inverse reconstruction after normalization. J1 covers the multidimensional Fourier transform. Multiple processes, distributed performance and other FFT executors are not verified by this example. Local exports require compatible MPICH and FFTW development libraries and locally appropriate include/link flags. GPL-3.0-or-later notices are retained. |
| nufft1d: `classic Fortran 90 interface` / `nufft1d1f90` | [J1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft1d/blob/42a04333f882f474b0674936cae049899784f56e/nufft1df90.f#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft1d/blob/42a04333f882f474b0674936cae049899784f56e/dfft.f#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft1d/blob/42a04333f882f474b0674936cae049899784f56e/nufft1d_demof90.f) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/nufft1d.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-spatial-nufft.md) | Original one-dimensional nonuniform Fourier code using the dynamically allocated Fortran 90 interface and bundled Netlib FFT implementation. Forty-nine checksum-guarded edits change legacy extent-one FFT dummy-array declarations to assumed size; every changed array is checked against its containing procedure argument list. No local arrays, transform formulas or global bounds-checking settings are changed. Legacy workspace argument type punning is accepted with the package-specific -fallow-argument-mismatch option. The example evaluates a type-1 transform of 96 deterministic nonuniform points into 32 modes for both exponent signs and compares every complex coefficient with an independent direct sum, including the documented 1/nj normalization. Status and finite values are checked. Types 2/3, the static Fortran 77 interface and every bundled FFT routine are not verified. The class-specific J1 driver adds 24 type-1 transforms: 80 nonuniform sources, 15/16/31/32 modes, three deterministic strength patterns and both exponent signs, checked against independently accumulated trigonometric sums and the exact zero mode. Type 2/3 and performance remain unverified. |
| nufft2d: `classic Fortran 90 interface` / `nufft2d1f90` | [J1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft2d/blob/8353c5fb20cd0a8fe6821fae18355e303ed23ed3/nufft2df90.f#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft2d/blob/8353c5fb20cd0a8fe6821fae18355e303ed23ed3/dfft.f#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/zgimbutas/nufft2d/blob/8353c5fb20cd0a8fe6821fae18355e303ed23ed3/nufft2d_demof90.f) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/nufft2d.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-moments-nufft2d.md) | Original two-dimensional nonuniform Fourier code using the dynamically allocated Fortran 90 interface and bundled Netlib FFT implementation. Forty-nine checksum-guarded edits change legacy extent-one FFT dummy-array declarations to assumed size after checking that each is a procedure argument; no formulas or local arrays change and bounds checking remains enabled. Legacy workspace argument type punning uses the package-specific -fallow-argument-mismatch option. The example transforms 96 deterministic scattered points onto a 16-by-18 mode grid for both exponent signs, checking status, finite values and all coefficients against an independent direct sum with the documented 1/nj normalization. Types 2/3, the static Fortran 77 interface, every FFT routine and multidimensional performance are not verified. |

The retained REAL64 probe fails at x=-6 against independently differentiated mpmath references, confirmed at 80/120 digits. For GELU=x*Phi(x), the derivative is Phi(x)+x*phi(x); the implementation uses x/sqrt(2) instead of x/sqrt(2*pi) for the exponential term. No checked gradient driver is published and no library source was patched. Exact source and full result: docs/gams-candidates/stdlib-activations/gelu-gradient-result.json. |

This does not certify the requested tolerance for all inputs or breakdown recovery. Right-hand sides and final residuals use scalar loops. Finite results and matrix preservation checked. Sparse convenience interfaces, zero RHS, boundary masks, arbitrary ill-conditioning, convergence diagnostics and speed unverified. |

Scaled absolute tolerance 3e-12, so tiny tails are not certified to relative accuracy. Noninteger shapes, negative x, extreme tails, integer-overload semantics and other kinds unverified. |

It imports the current int_m conversion API explicitly. This is not arbitrary-precision arithmetic or validation of every kind, operation or C interoperability path. |

This N2 driver does not mark the separate A1 mapping verified. |

Other complex arithmetic is outside this driver. |

This is fixed-capacity decimal representation, not unlimited arbitrary precision. Dedicated A1 driver checks 3362 signed small additions/subtractions against native integer results and five exact large-decimal identities beyond int64, including carry, borrow, ordering and cancellation. Fixed capacity and overflow are not validated. |

## Integral transforms

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| spherepack: `type_Sphere` / `Sphere%invert_helmholtz` | [I2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I2) | problem-specific source or delayed derivative callback | [Source](https://github.com/jlokimlin/spherepack/blob/e80da29462c5987162a0884baaab7434d0215467/src/type_Sphere.f90#L748) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jlokimlin/spherepack/blob/e80da29462c5987162a0884baaab7434d0215467/examples/helmsph.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/spherepack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-helmholtz-streams.md) | Spherical Helmholtz inversion using harmonic analysis and synthesis; two vector-workspace rank declarations are patched in the catalog recipe. Dedicated I2 driver checks 258 nonresonant Helmholtz and zero-mean Poisson solves against exact real spherical-harmonic solutions on Gaussian and regular grids. Ten zonal/nonzonal modes through degree three and their mixture are used at three resolutions; no vector PDE or resonant-shift claim. |
| spherepack: `type_GaussianSphere` / `GaussianSphere%perform_scalar_analysis / perform_scalar_synthesis` | [J](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J) | problem-specific source or delayed derivative callback | [Source](https://github.com/jlokimlin/spherepack/blob/e80da29462c5987162a0884baaab7434d0215467/src/type_GaussianSphere.f90#L183) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jlokimlin/spherepack/blob/e80da29462c5987162a0884baaab7434d0215467/examples/helmsph.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/spherepack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-quantities-spherical.md) | Spherical harmonic analysis and synthesis on Gaussian grids; no narrower trigonometric-transform subclass is inferred. Dedicated J consumer verifies P0..P4 zonal fields and a mixed zonal field on three Gaussian grids. Analyzed coefficients must match independently normalized Legendre amplitudes, valid nonzonal coefficients must vanish, and synthesis is checked both after analysis and after erasing/injecting exact coefficients. Nonzero nonzonal modes, vector transforms, Nyquist/invalid coefficient storage and Helmholtz/PDE routines are not verified. |

The checked driver adapts author test_qags (1/sqrt(x)) and adds log(x)/sqrt(x). These endpoint singularities have exact integrals 2 and -4. It checks status zero, finite values, independent accuracy and callback counts; it does not evaluate the singular endpoint or claim coverage of all special integrands. |

| scifortran: `SF_INTEGRATE` / `simps / trapz / quad` | [H2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/H2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/SciFortran/SciFortran/blob/ca4a55f49cfdf7a94e388bc3475a5db574d5de99/src/SF_INTEGRATE/SF_INTEGRATE.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/SciFortran/SciFortran/blob/ca4a55f49cfdf7a94e388bc3475a5db574d5de99/src/SF_INTEGRATE/integrate_sample_1d.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/SciFortran/SciFortran/blob/ca4a55f49cfdf7a94e388bc3475a5db574d5de99/src/SF_INTEGRATE/integrate_quad_func.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/SciFortran/SciFortran/blob/ca4a55f49cfdf7a94e388bc3475a5db574d5de99/test/src/SF_INTEGRATE/01_test_sf_integrate_quad.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/scifortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-roots-splines-quadrature.md) | Selected integration subset, explicitly labeled in the menu: SF_INTEGRATE, GAUSS_QUADRATURE and standalone bundled QUADPACK procedures. Original include files remain in their source directories and the operator FPM wrapper compiles only the listed library files. Checksum-guarded edits promote 478 long coefficient literals from E to D exponents in DATA statements and REAL(8) PARAMETER array initializers in two bundled QUADPACK files. Each target array is checked to be REAL(8), and all decimal digits are retained. This avoids evaluating the supplied coefficients at default-real precision before assignment to double precision; formulas and the default-real compiler mode are unchanged. The linked quadrature-fortran BSD-3-Clause notice is restored as comments in GAUSS_QUADRATURE so source exports retain it. The consumer checks the Simpson and trapezoidal integrals of a sampled cubic against 1/4, adaptive integration of exp(x) over [0,1] against e-1 and exp(-x) over [0,infinity) against 1, and rejects nonfinite results. All six finite-interval Gauss-Kronrod rule choices are also checked against e-1. The example uses module callbacks: an initial consumer with internal callbacks segfaulted in the sandbox. Other SciFortran modules, MPI, linear algebra, the SCIFOR umbrella module, multidimensional/vector quadrature and all integration options are outside this selected build and verification scope. Dedicated H2 consumer verifies 60 sampled polynomial fixtures through four Simpson/trapezoid routes (240 calls), using exact integrals and analytic trapezoidal corrections. Checks 178 adaptive calls: polynomial antiderivatives with default/six Gauss-Kronrod rules, exponential tails, Gaussian/Cauchy whole-line integrals and endpoint-root singularities. Uses module callbacks and existing coefficient-precision repairs. Weighted/pole integrals, complex/nonuniform sampled rules, all error paths and performance are outside this driver. |

| delaunaysparse: `DELSPARSE_MOD` / `DELAUNAYSPARSES` | [E2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/vtopt/DelaunaySparse/blob/f3ffef04e24281aa42a0e5ec81f5c68f6bb5fb01/src/delsparse.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/vtopt/DelaunaySparse/blob/f3ffef04e24281aa42a0e5ec81f5c68f6bb5fb01/src/test_install.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/delaunaysparse.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-spatial-nufft.md) | Version 1 multivariate interpolation using sparse Delaunay simplices. Version 2 bundles BQPD with a research-only redistribution notice, so the selected pin uses the earlier MIT-licensed version 1 with its bundled SLATEC/BLAS/LAPACK sources. An FPM wrapper retains fixed/free source forms, legacy implicit typing/interfaces and -fallow-argument-mismatch for the SLATEC scalar-zero/zero-stride BLAS convention. OpenMP is enabled to link the library; the checked driver uses the serial routine. It verifies exact linear-function reproduction at three interior 2D points, zero status codes, valid simplex indices and barycentric weight sums/signs. No numerical source edits. Convex-hull projection/extrapolation, all degenerate inputs, high-dimensional behavior and parallel variants are not validated. The class-specific E2 driver verifies six 3D interior queries with ten irregular points, three output fields, affine/constant reproduction, unique nondegenerate simplex vertices and barycentric reconstruction of preserved original coordinates. The nonlinear field is checked as piecewise-linear interpolation; exact nonlinear-function recovery and the empty-circumsphere property are not certified. |

The main checks exact interpolation of a linear function, the expected interpolation error for a quadratic, an exact grid value, and clamping below/above the endpoints. No mathematical source edits. The 2D interface, nonuniform spacing options, persistence and all boundary cases are not validated by this example. Linear grids only: independent two-stage interpolation references check 98 queries on original/copied and updated tables, three columns, four lookup routes, location coordinates, endpoint clamping and column resampling/addition. This E3 service driver does not verify the separate E1a mapping. |
| pchip: `pchip_module` / `dpchsp, dpchfd` | [E1a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E1a) | first derivatives of fitted spline | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/src/pchip_module.f90#L2456) · [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/src/pchip_module.f90#L1216) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/test/pchip_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/pchip.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-transforms-structures-ten.md) | 225 REAL64 cubic reproduction queries: three shifted cubics, six nonuniform nodes, and not-a-knot/supplied first derivative/supplied second derivative endpoint conditions. DPCHSP constructs slopes; DPCHFD values and first derivatives must match independent explicit cubic formulas at 25 points per case. Natural zero-second-derivative conditions are not imposed on nonmatching cubics. Extrapolation and noisy-data claims excluded. |
| pchip: `pchip_module` / `dpchim, dpchfe` | [E1a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E1a) | none | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/src/pchip_module.f90#L2216) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/test/pchip_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/pchip.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-interpolation.md) | Shape-preserving piecewise cubic Hermite interpolation; slopes are estimated from tabulated values. |
| pchip: `pchip_module` / `dpchia, dpchid` | [E3a3](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E3a3) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/src/pchip_module.f90#L1652) · [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/src/pchip_module.f90#L2085) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/PCHIP/blob/8f5350565072622a2344dd0ec75193832b31eb10/test/pchip_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/pchip.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-transforms-structures-ten.md) | 117 REAL64 piecewise cubic Hermite integrals on a six-node nonuniform grid with explicitly supplied dyadic values/slopes. 81 arbitrary-bound and 36 node-index intervals include zero/reversed/multi-panel bounds. Independent per-panel two-point Gauss integration of Hermite basis polynomials, plus saved Python Fraction antiderivatives. The slopes are caller-supplied; this does not test DPCHIM slope selection. Extrapolation excluded. |
| regridpack: `regridpack_module` / `regrid` | [E1a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E1a) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/regridpack/blob/e47346d9244ab93f70367f16364d73217fbc0451/src/regridpack_module.f90#L22) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/regridpack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-regrid-rk45.md) | One-dimensional piecewise linear/cubic regridding overload; multidimensional overloads require separate review. |

Dedicated K1 driver checks three known affine least-squares fits, including duplicate-coordinate opposite noise and a known nonzero SSE of 1.5, plus nonuniform-weight constant data. Values and analytic derivatives are checked at 21 points per fit. Cubic/nonlinear fitting accuracy, multidimensional fits, extrapolation and ill-conditioning are outside this driver. |

## Linear algebra

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| arpack-ng: `classic Fortran interface` / `DSAUPD / DSEUPD` | [D4a1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D4a1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/opencollab/arpack-ng/blob/66faef9e09bb35f5acafe02960885050acc46fa0/SRC/dsaupd.f#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/opencollab/arpack-ng/blob/66faef9e09bb35f5acafe02960885050acc46fa0/SRC/dseupd.f#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/opencollab/arpack-ng/blob/66faef9e09bb35f5acafe02960885050acc46fa0/EXAMPLES/SYM/dsdrv1.f) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/arpack-ng.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-linear-algebra.md) | Serial classic Fortran ARPACK-NG interfaces, built from unchanged SRC .f files, the documented UTIL support set, and upstream debug/statistics initializers. The wrapper uses native LP64 BLAS/LAPACK and excludes PARPACK/MPI and generated C bindings. The upstream second_NONE timing stub disables ARPACK internal elapsed-time statistics; playground build/run timings still work. Adapted from the author symmetric reverse-communication example, the main computes three smallest eigenpairs of a 20-by-20 symmetric tridiagonal operator, checks convergence status/count, analytical eigenvalues and eigenvector residual norms. A deterministic nonzero initial residual avoids dependence on random initialization. Nonsymmetric/complex modes, shift-invert, generalized problems and parallel execution are not validated. Dedicated D4 driver checks the three smallest and three largest eigenpairs of the 20-by-20 Dirichlet tridiagonal (2 diagonal,-1 off-diagonal), with explicit starting residual, convergence/extraction status, analytical eigenvalues and sign-invariant sine eigenvector comparisons, equation residuals and orthonormality. Generalized, nonsymmetric, shift-invert and arbitrary spectra are not verified. Refined from broad D4 to D4a1 for the reviewed real symmetric ordinary-eigenproblem interfaces. |
| blas-interface: `blas` / `blas_gemm` | [D1b6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D1b6) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/awvwgk/blas-interface/blob/552a2e6a41443843003216fedad6419af55f8e35/src/blas/level3.f90#L35) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/blas-interface.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-linear-algebra.md) | Matrix-matrix multiplication interface to external BLAS, not a standalone BLAS implementation. Dedicated D1b6 driver checks 12 rectangular real64 GEMM calls against independent scalar triple loops, covering all N/T input combinations and alpha/beta pairs (1,0), (-2,0.5), (0,-1). Padded leading dimensions, complex overloads and other BLAS routines are not checked. |
| dbcsr: `dbcsr_api` / `dbcsr_multiply` | [D1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/cp2k/dbcsr/blob/cd6f522ec5005da8c59d2883975a89a2da607eac/src/mm/dbcsr_multiply_api.F#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/cp2k/dbcsr/blob/cd6f522ec5005da8c59d2883975a89a2da607eac/src/block/dbcsr_block_access.F#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/cp2k/dbcsr/blob/cd6f522ec5005da8c59d2883975a89a2da607eac/src/dbcsr_api.F#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/cp2k/dbcsr/blob/cd6f522ec5005da8c59d2883975a89a2da607eac/examples/dbcsr_example_3.F) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/dbcsr.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-tangents-blocks.md) | The upstream CMake-selected serial CPU library is rendered with Fypp 3.2, with exact generated-output hashes. Simple USE lists emitted from Python sets are sorted for reproducibility across Python versions and platforms; numerical algorithms are unchanged. MPI, OpenMP, GPU, LIBXS and LIBXSMM are disabled, and the library links pinned OpenBLAS. The diagnostic filename macro uses __FILE__. The checked main creates two 4-by-4 block-sparse matrices, multiplies them through dbcsr_multiply, reconstructs the result and independently compares it with MATMUL, requiring finite entries and a small maximum error. D1 covers matrix operations. Parallel scaling, accelerators and tensor operations are outside this verification scope. GPL-2.0-or-later notices are retained. The wrapper repeats the base-hooks USE list so FPM can order dependencies hidden in the original include. Its manifest permits legacy implicit external BLAS/LAPACK calls; runtime and bounds checks remain enabled. |

Other methods, cutoff selection, ill-conditioning and near-threshold ranks are not verified. |

| linalg_fortran: `linalg_fortran` / `linalg%eigh` | [D4a1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D4a1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/Euler-37/linalg_fortran/blob/30e7237ab54ca176e1c8dae4be2d9d44800e1f83/src/linalg_fortran.f90#L672) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/linalg_fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-linear-algebra.md) | Typed LAPACK interfaces. The checked real64 main verifies a matrix inverse residual, determinant, known eigenvalues, eigenpair residuals and orthonormality for a symmetric 3-by-3 tridiagonal matrix. Complex, single-precision and other BLAS/LAPACK wrappers are not covered. Legacy implicit external interfaces are explicitly enabled in the pinned package manifest. Dedicated D4 driver checks all 20 eigenpairs of the same Dirichlet tridiagonal matrix used by the ARPACK driver against analytic eigenvalues and sign-invariant sine eigenvectors, with residuals, orthonormality and reconstruction. This covers a real symmetric ordinary problem only; no generic solver success status is inferred from the API return. Refined from broad D4 to D4a1 for the reviewed real symmetric ordinary-eigenproblem interfaces. |
| linalg_fortran: `linalg_fortran` / `linalg%inv / linalg%det` | [D2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/Euler-37/linalg_fortran/blob/30e7237ab54ca176e1c8dae4be2d9d44800e1f83/src/linalg_fortran.f90#L489) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/Euler-37/linalg_fortran/blob/30e7237ab54ca176e1c8dae4be2d9d44800e1f83/test/check.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/linalg_fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-inverse-tvd.md) | Typed LAPACK interfaces. The checked real64 main verifies a matrix inverse residual, determinant, known eigenvalues, eigenpair residuals and orthonormality for a symmetric 3-by-3 tridiagonal matrix. Complex, single-precision and other BLAS/LAPACK wrappers are not covered. Legacy implicit external interfaces are explicitly enabled in the pinned package manifest. Dedicated D2 real64 consumer checks 132 scaled known LDU matrices and first/last-row permutations, using closed-form bidiagonal inverse factors and determinant products. Both left/right inverse residuals are accumulated with independent scalar loops. Two exact rank-deficient examples must return a false inverse status and zero determinant. Ill-conditioned/near-singular, nonsquare, complex and real32 cases are not verified. |

| ntpoly: `InverseSolversModule` / `Invert` | [D2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/william-dawson/NTPoly/blob/7b3d22f76226af1402e9b0a5be18a496e4addc28/Source/Fortran/InverseSolversModule.F90#L29) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/william-dawson/NTPoly/blob/7b3d22f76226af1402e9b0a5be18a496e4addc28/Examples/PremadeMatrix/main.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/ntpoly.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-gmres-inverse.md) | The upstream Fortran library source set is wrapped for FPM with pinned MPICH and OpenBLAS native dependencies. The verified example uses exactly one MPI process, serialized MPI thread support and shared-memory/self UCX transports in the restricted sandbox. For a real symmetric positive-definite 2-by-2 matrix with eigenvalues 1 and 9, it checks SquareRoot, InverseSquareRoot and Invert against analytic matrices and independent product residuals, rejecting nonfinite values. D covers matrix functions and D2 covers inversion. Multiple MPI processes, distributed scaling, complex matrices and other solver families are outside this verification scope. Local exports require MPI/OpenBLAS development libraries and locally appropriate compiler/linker flags. |
| ntpoly: `SquareRootSolversModule` / `SquareRoot / InverseSquareRoot` | [D](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/william-dawson/NTPoly/blob/7b3d22f76226af1402e9b0a5be18a496e4addc28/Source/Fortran/SquareRootSolversModule.F90#L30) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/william-dawson/NTPoly/blob/7b3d22f76226af1402e9b0a5be18a496e4addc28/Examples/PremadeMatrix/main.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/ntpoly.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-matrix-roots-box.md) | The upstream Fortran library source set is wrapped for FPM with pinned MPICH and OpenBLAS native dependencies. The verified example uses exactly one MPI process, serialized MPI thread support and shared-memory/self UCX transports in the restricted sandbox. For a real symmetric positive-definite 2-by-2 matrix with eigenvalues 1 and 9, it checks SquareRoot, InverseSquareRoot and Invert against analytic matrices and independent product residuals, rejecting nonfinite values. D covers matrix functions and D2 covers inversion. Multiple MPI processes, distributed scaling, complex matrices and other solver families are outside this verification scope. Local exports require MPI/OpenBLAS development libraries and locally appropriate compiler/linker flags. Dedicated D consumer checks 24 known SPD matrices in dimensions 2/3 and their principal square/inverse-square roots against independently constructed orthogonal-factor formulas. Three rotations and four positive-root pairs give 48 matrix-function calls; scalar products check root squared, inverse-root congruence and mutual inverse identity. Exactly one MPI process. Does not verify the separate Invert/D2 API, distributed scaling, complex/indefinite/singular matrices or large sparse behavior. |
| propack: `classic double-precision interface` / `dlansvd` | [D6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D6) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/scipy/PROPACK/blob/36f95269ecee6ae42af0c00707b3c3cf3f869a35/double/dlansvd.F#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/scipy/PROPACK/blob/36f95269ecee6ae42af0c00707b3c3cf3f869a35/double/dlanbpro.F#L1) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/propack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-propack-fmm.md) | Historical SciPy PROPACK 2.1 sources, whose repository is archived and explicitly points to rmlarsen/propack for the maintained implementation. The current author archive and tags v2.2/v2.1 exceed the unchanged source download limit, largely due to saved example outputs. The menu explicitly identifies this historical source choice. Only the native double-precision serial routines are wrapped, using the portable RISC modified Gram-Schmidt implementation and compatible system LP64 BLAS/LAPACK. Numerical sources are unchanged. A callback supplies multiplication by a rectangular diagonal matrix with known singular values. The consumer passes a writable convergence-count argument, requests the leading three singular triplets, and checks 12, 11 and 10, both A*v-sigma*u and transpose(A)*u-sigma*v residuals, finite values and unit vector norms. The restarted algorithm, other precisions, arbitrary sparse matrices and parallel performance are not verified. |

| scalapackfx: `scalapackfx_module` / `scalafx_psyev` | [D4a1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D4a1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/dftbplus/scalapackfx/blob/570cbf45836ed05cf791bc3f404f4f10929e5017/lib/scalapackfx.fpp#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/dftbplus/scalapackfx/blob/570cbf45836ed05cf791bc3f404f4f10929e5017/lib/scalapackfx.fypp#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/dftbplus/scalapackfx/blob/570cbf45836ed05cf791bc3f404f4f10929e5017/lib/blacsgrid.fpp#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/dftbplus/scalapackfx/blob/570cbf45836ed05cf791bc3f404f4f10929e5017/test/test_diag.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/scalapackfx.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-sparse-spectra.md) | The upstream CMake library source list is generated with Fypp 3.2 and exact generated-output checksums. The example uses a 1-by-1 BLACS grid and pinned MPICH, ScaLAPACK and OpenBLAS native libraries. It computes eigenvalues and eigenvectors of a symmetric 3-by-3 tridiagonal matrix using scalafx_psyev, checks eigenvalues against 2-sqrt(2), 2 and 2+sqrt(2), and independently checks eigenpair residuals and orthogonality. All entries must be finite. D4a1 (ordinary real symmetric eigenproblems) applies to the tested eigensolver. This is a one-process API example, not a distributed-performance demonstration; other solvers, generalized eigenproblems and multiple processes are not verified. |

The generator sorts the imported layer routine names for reproducibility; network operations are unchanged. Four inputs exercise positive and clipped hidden units and predictions are checked against independently evaluated weights and biases, including negative and positive outputs and finiteness. K covers inference as approximation; no least-squares fitting or training classification is inferred. The sample network is illustrative, not a trained scientific model. Other network layers, external model conversion and training are not verified. BSD-3-Clause and Crown copyright notices are retained. The dedicated GAMS driver additionally checks generic dense/activation pipelines with 1/2/5 channel rows and five activations against 35,200 scalar references, and the existing generated model on an 81-point grid covering all four ReLU regions. Extreme-logit softmax and external model conversion remain unverified. |

Based on the author's write-read-infer example, the checked consumer constructs a two-input ReLU network with known diagonal weights and biases, evaluates negative, zero and positive input pairs, and compares every output with direct formulas. K is a broad function-approximation classification; training algorithms, gradients, model-file round trips and parallel speedups are not verified. The historical version is explicit in the menu description. The original Berkeley Lab license terms, including the enhancements clause, are retained. The dedicated GAMS driver additionally checks 48 fixed multilayer networks with irregular widths, three activations, scalar references, padding sentinels and repeated inference; default identity input/output ranges only. |

The operator C++ flag setting is restricted to a current, unshared source-package recipe; public requests cannot set it and previous cache keys are preserved. The self-contained consumer embeds a reproducibly generated 1,766-byte TorchScript model as hexadecimal text, writes tiny.pt in the sandbox and calls the author tensor/model API in REAL64. Two different five-element inputs exercise y=2*x+1, with each output checked independently for finiteness and absolute error below 1e-12. The second call also verifies that the input tensor observes updated Fortran storage. No Python or model download runs in visitor jobs. This is an illustrative affine inference model, not a trained scientific predictor. K covers inference as function approximation; no training or optimization classification is inferred. GPU use, training, autograd, parallel execution and arbitrary external models are not verified. FTorch MIT notices and native Debian dependency notices are retained. APT estimates about 924 MB of additional native dependencies in the isolated FTorch image; these are separate from the catalog logical library-artifact total. Dedicated K driver now checks the same fixed affine model across REAL32/REAL64 tensor ranks 1/2/3, asymmetric dimension permutations, metadata, in-place input updates and replay. It verifies 1742 exact dyadic output entries; nonlinear models, gradients and training remain unverified. |

| fgsl: `fgsl` / `fgsl_integration_qags` | [H2a1a1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/H2a1a1) | none | [Source](https://github.com/reinh-bader/fgsl/blob/27a3e7cc0809623bcfde680c39a6fdcf0f590c4c/api/integration.finc#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/reinh-bader/fgsl/blob/27a3e7cc0809623bcfde680c39a6fdcf0f590c4c/interface/integration.finc#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/reinh-bader/fgsl/blob/27a3e7cc0809623bcfde680c39a6fdcf0f590c4c/fgsl.F90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/reinh-bader/fgsl/blob/27a3e7cc0809623bcfde680c39a6fdcf0f590c4c/doc/examples/integration.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fgsl.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-rkf-qags.md) | FGSL wraps GSL numerical routines. Broad classifications follow the exported interfaces for special functions, linear algebra, interpolation, root finding, optimization, integration/differentiation, ODEs, transforms, fitting and statistics. The checked consumer adapts the author's QAGS example and independently verifies the integral of log(x)/sqrt(x) on [0,1] against -4, status and error estimate. The procedure mapping covers adaptive one-dimensional finite-interval quadrature; other numerical interfaces are not runtime-verified by this example. Upstream Autoconf probes generated the complete configuration on GNU/Linux LP64 with GSL 2.8. An audited asset stages that header as config.inc; checksum-guarded edits change only its include filename in Fortran and C. The C support object and pinned system GSL libraries are linked. Local exports include the asset and patches; this configuration targets GNU/Linux LP64, and another ABI or GSL version requires regeneration against its local tools. GPL-2.0-or-later notices are retained. |

The main sorts repeated and negative integers, builds a k-d tree for 20 deterministic 2D points and compares the nearest point with an independent direct search. The OpenMP metapackage is resolved explicitly and -fopenmp is enabled; the execution sandbox still uses one thread. No source edits. The broad library is compiled, but this driver does not validate all containers, random generators, mathematical utilities or parallel performance. A broader nearest-neighbor probe fails on a valid 31-point query; its source, hash and result are retained in docs/gams-candidates/coretran-nearest. This procedure has no checked GAMS driver. |

The main sorts repeated and negative integers, builds a k-d tree for 20 deterministic 2D points and compares the nearest point with an independent direct search. The OpenMP metapackage is resolved explicitly and -fopenmp is enabled; the execution sandbox still uses one thread. No source edits. The broad library is compiled, but this driver does not validate all containers, random generators, mathematical utilities or parallel performance. The checked GAMS driver uses explicit stable=true only. A mixed-setting probe fails with stable=false on 16 equal integer32 values; source/hash/result are retained in docs/gams-candidates/coretran-sort. |
| fmm2d: `classic Fortran interface` / `rfmm2d_t_c_p` | [D1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D1), [I2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/I2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/flatironinstitute/fmm2d/blob/7e7509ecd6ebe31b9292cb00825410b478f44808/src/laplace/rfmm2dwrap.f#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/flatironinstitute/fmm2d/blob/7e7509ecd6ebe31b9292cb00825410b478f44808/src/laplace/cfmm2d.f#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/flatironinstitute/fmm2d/blob/7e7509ecd6ebe31b9292cb00825410b478f44808/test/laplace/test_rfmm2d.f) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fmm2d.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-propack-fmm.md) | Native Fortran FMM2D source set selected from the upstream makefile with optional C++ fast kernels disabled. OpenMP and portable -O2 are enabled; the visitor sandbox uses one execution thread and retains bounds checks. Checksum-guarded compatibility edits skip zero-point multipole/local evaluations and zero-source neighbor calls before passing one-past-end array elements. The inverse reorder helper declares its arrays with the actual n extent rather than the legacy dummy extent 1. Source and target branches receive matching guards; formulas are unchanged. The checked real Laplace driver evaluates 512 deterministic sources at 16 separate targets and compares with an independent charge-weighted log-distance sum. A separate source-and-target probe also checks all source potentials by direct summation with self-interactions excluded, exercising the source-side guards. The code is classified as a structured kernel-matrix application and Green-function evaluation related to PDEs, not as a complete boundary-value solver. Helmholtz, biharmonic/Stokes, dipoles, gradients/Hessians, every complex/vector interface and parallel speedups are not validated. The small accuracy check is not a performance benchmark. |

A single-image coarray build is used. The checked consumer adapts the author's extended Gauss-divergence example to f=x**2/2 and v=x on a 64-cell unit interval. It checks the two volume integrals against 1/3 and 1/6, the boundary flux against 1/2, finiteness and the conservation residual using single-precision tolerances. The command-line parsing and shell display calls of the upstream example are omitted. Library numerical sources are unchanged. Other 2D/3D operators, time integration and convergence orders are not validated. Original Berkeley Lab license terms, including the enhancements clause, are retained; this is not labeled plain BSD-3-Clause. |
| fortran-utils: `splines / mesh / sorting` / `spline3ders / meshexp / sort` | [E1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E1), [N6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/N6) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/certik/fortran-utils/blob/b43bd24cd421509a5bc6d3b9c3eeae8ce856ed88/src/splines.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/certik/fortran-utils/blob/b43bd24cd421509a5bc6d3b9c3eeae8ce856ed88/src/mesh.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/certik/fortran-utils/blob/b43bd24cd421509a5bc6d3b9c3eeae8ce856ed88/src/sorting.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/certik/fortran-utils/blob/b43bd24cd421509a5bc6d3b9c3eeae8ce856ed88/tests/splines/test_splines.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortran-utils.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-roots-splines-quadrature.md) | Selected unmodified modules types, utils, lapack, mesh, splines and sorting, wrapped for FPM and linked to compatible LP64 BLAS/LAPACK. HDF5, AMOS/special functions, optimization and other unselected modules are not included. The main reproduces a cubic polynomial with the consistent-boundary cubic spline and checks its value, first derivative and second derivative on a denser grid against exact formulas. It checks exponential-mesh endpoints, monotonicity and the requested last/first spacing ratio, then sorts repeated/negative integers against an exact answer. General nonpolynomial interpolation error, extrapolation, every boundary condition, other sorting interfaces and all utility routines are not validated. Dedicated E1 consumer checks exact degree-0..3 polynomial reproduction on three uniform grids and a nonuniform grid, including first/second derivatives at 65 queries. Optional outputs and reversed query order are compared independently. Uses the API default consistent-cubic endpoint conditions; extrapolation, nonpolynomial convergence, other boundary choices and the separate N6 sorting mapping are not verified. |
| fortranlib: `lib_algebra / lib_array` / `quadratic / linspace / integral / interp1d` | [E1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/E1), [F1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/F1), [H2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/H2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/astrofrog/fortranlib/blob/60fd094d3e137137ce00ec120b085e9b23ebf68b/src/lib_algebra.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/astrofrog/fortranlib/blob/60fd094d3e137137ce00ec120b085e9b23ebf68b/src/lib_array.f90#L1) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortranlib.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-roots-splines-quadrature.md) | Selected base_types, lib_algebra and lib_array modules, using the pinned revision immediately before the upstream FPM test suite introduced source symlinks. Nine checksum-guarded edits bring lib_array exactly to upstream commit 844611933cba06d71490033e68b355f6f0ff704f, retaining its integration-limit, descending histogram-bin and precision fixes. The other selected modules are unchanged. The ordinary archive policy remains unchanged. A synthetic FPM wrapper uses the already generated Fortran sources, not the Python templates. The checked consumer integrates the affine function 2*x+1 on [0,1], checks linear interpolation at an interior off-grid point, and solves x**2-5*x+6 against the exact roots 2 and 3. Additional checks cover integration limits outside the tabulated range, descending histogram bins and bin-center precision; the expanded example fails with the older unpatched array module and passes with the upstream fixes. GAMS F1 denotes the quadratic solver, not a general iterative root finder. Random sampling, statistics, geometry, HDF5/FITS bindings and other modules are not selected; the example does not validate every routine in the included modules. Dedicated F1 consumer verifies 84 real64 quadratic calls from 21 distinct dyadic root pairs and four signed coefficient scales, comparing sorted roots, root sums/products and normalized residuals. Uses only the ordinary quadratic API, not Pascal/reduced variants. Repeated/complex roots, zero leading coefficient, severe cancellation and extreme scaling are not verified. The separate E1 interpolation and H2 integration mappings remain source-supported. |

The main verifies resistor and capacitor impedances and their series combination against direct complex formulas at four angular frequencies. Kinetics and parameter estimation are not covered. A4 classifies the complex arithmetic interface, not an electrochemistry-specific GAMS class. Dedicated A4 consumer checks R, C and L at 216 frequency cases over 12 parameter sets, with finite complex results, status/message checks and normalized closed-form errors. Series RLC resonance and parallel RC combinations are formed by the driver, not a package circuit solver. Nonideal elements, fitting, kinetics and exceptional inputs are not verified. |

A checksum-guarded adapter exposes the original internal procedures as module playground_kronrod, removes the large driver loop and declares app as the library directory; The consumer uses the wrapped module instead of running the original batch drivers. Procedure bodies, constants and precision settings are unchanged. The main generates the symmetric 7-point rule, checks six even polynomial integrals through degree 10 at 40-decimal-place tolerance with MPFUN arithmetic, and prints double-precision summaries. Only the unchanged MPFUN dependency is shared. This is a rule generator, not an adaptive integrator or full validation of every rule/order. |

OpenMP directives are inactive in this serial profile and OpenBLAS supplies dense linear algebra. The checked consumer adapts the author main and formaldehyde input, embedding its four-atom coordinates, radii, charges and solver settings. It compares solvation energy and all force components against the author reference output, requires finite values and checks zero net force. Full-sized dummy arrays replace the unused one-element placeholders in the example while leaving library numerical routines unchanged. D2 identifies the Jacobi/DIIS linear-system solution used by cosmo; Z denotes the physical solvation model. Other molecules, solvent parameters, quantum densities and parallel performance are outside this verification scope. LGPL notices and author reference requests remain in the source tree. Dedicated D2 driver checks jacobi_diis directly on 192 manufactured strictly diagonally dominant systems with true residuals, four norm modes, two guesses and plain Jacobi/default DIIS(25). A history-4 bounds failure from the ten-entry eviction code is retained in docs/gams-candidates/ddpcm-diis-history; no library patch is applied. These direct solver checks do not extend the molecular verification scope. |

First-six returned wavefunctions are checked only for finiteness. Dirac/DFT branches, wavefunction normalization and full-spectrum accuracy are not verified. |
| libmbd: `mbd / mbd_hamiltonian` / `mbd_calc_t%evaluate_vdw_method / get_mbd_hamiltonian_energy_real` | [D4](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D4) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/libmbd/libmbd/blob/77d7ab424d21f43dea9f57734b355ce4ea4d1a42/src/mbd.F90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/libmbd/libmbd/blob/77d7ab424d21f43dea9f57734b355ce4ea4d1a42/src/mbd_hamiltonian.F90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/libmbd/libmbd/blob/77d7ab424d21f43dea9f57734b355ce4ea4d1a42/src/mbd_matrix.F90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/libmbd/libmbd/blob/77d7ab424d21f43dea9f57734b355ce4ea4d1a42/tests/mbd_api_tests.F90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/libmbd.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-dispersion.md) | The serial Fortran library and Fortran-written C interface are selected from the upstream CMake target and linked with pinned OpenBLAS. MPI/ScaLAPACK and ELSI are disabled. An audited version module uses an explicitly identified 0.0.0 plus pinned-commit suffix for the Git archive, rather than claiming a release version. The checked main adapts the author's argon-dimer tests at four angstrom separation, verifies dispersion energy and gradient norm against upstream reference values, checks finite results and equal/opposite gradients, and checks library exceptions. D4 covers the Hamiltonian diagonalization used by the many-body dispersion calculation; Z denotes the domain-specific physical model, not a general-purpose eigensolver interface. Other molecules, periodic systems, distributed runs and all available models are outside this consumer verification scope. MPL-2.0 notices are retained. |

| rng-fortran: `m_random` / `rng_t%normal / poisson / circle / sphere` | [L6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L6) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jannisteunissen/rng_fortran/blob/5ead95f436323d07fc82acc3613f66550947f256/m_random.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jannisteunissen/rng_fortran/blob/5ead95f436323d07fc82acc3613f66550947f256/example.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rng-fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-raster-distributions.md) | Xoroshiro128+ pseudo-random generator. An FPM wrapper selects unchanged m_random.f90 and uses -fwrapv for its intentional signed-integer wraparound. Two fresh generators replay 10000 seeded uniform draws exactly; the main checks the implementation range [0,1), broad uniform/normal/Poisson(4) moment tolerances, nonnegative counts and unit circle/sphere norms. These are deterministic smoke checks, not statistical quality or cryptographic validation. OpenMP, jump independence, large Poisson rates and all distributions are not covered. A separate hosted reproducer confirms that set_seed does not clear the cached second normal after an odd normal() call; use a fresh generator to restart normal sequences. The selected replay uses fresh objects. Dedicated L6 consumer checks exact replay with fresh seeded objects, 60000 normal and 60000 small-rate Poisson samples against broad analytic moment/zero-probability tolerances, and 3000 circle plus 3000 sphere samples against exact norm references and broad coordinate moments. These are fixed-seed deterministic smoke checks, not statistical or cryptographic quality certification. Fresh allocations avoid the known cached-normal reseeding issue; that issue remains unresolved. Poisson rates >=10/rejection sampling, parallel/jump independence and full distribution coverage are not verified. |
| rng-fortran: `m_random` / `rng_t%set_seed / unif_01` | [L6a21](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L6a21), [L6c](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L6c) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jannisteunissen/rng_fortran/blob/5ead95f436323d07fc82acc3613f66550947f256/m_random.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jannisteunissen/rng_fortran/blob/5ead95f436323d07fc82acc3613f66550947f256/example.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rng-fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-helmholtz-streams.md) | Xoroshiro128+ pseudo-random generator. An FPM wrapper selects unchanged m_random.f90 and uses -fwrapv for its intentional signed-integer wraparound. Two fresh generators replay 10000 seeded uniform draws exactly; the main checks the implementation range [0,1), broad uniform/normal/Poisson(4) moment tolerances, nonnegative counts and unit circle/sphere norms. These are deterministic smoke checks, not statistical quality or cryptographic validation. OpenMP, jump independence, large Poisson rates and all distributions are not covered. A separate hosted reproducer confirms that set_seed does not clear the cached second normal after an odd normal() call; use a fresh generator to restart normal sequences. The selected replay uses fresh objects. Dedicated L6a21 driver checks 15 interleaved streams against unsigned-Python prefixes and rolling hashes, exact 52-bit integer/uniform conversion, seeded restarts and zero/default/two explicit jumps. Jump references use independent GF(2) transition-matrix exponentiation to 2**64. This establishes fixed bitstream and seed/jump API behavior, not statistical quality or thread independence; the cached-normal reseeding defect remains unresolved. |
| rngff: `rngff` / `linear_congruential_t%next` | [L6a21](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L6a21) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/Archaeologic-Inc/rngff/blob/8eccd1479f35159922a85db25a39bcd58a0c3faa/src/rngff/linear_congruential_m.f90#L14) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/Archaeologic-Inc/rngff/blob/8eccd1479f35159922a85db25a39bcd58a0c3faa/test/lcg_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rngff.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-random.md) | Combined linear congruential generator with explicit two-seed state. The real64 overload widens a real32 draw. The checked driver independently evaluates both modular recurrences in int64, verifies states and real32-rounded outputs for 1000 steps, and checks exact replay. |

## Roots and optimization

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| conmax: `conmax_module` / `slnpro` | [G2a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G2a) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/conmax/blob/ab7f237d30f94f7d13b86997e0c49f3d67bd7c54/src/conmax.f90#L1912) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/conmax/blob/ab7f237d30f94f7d13b86997e0c49f3d67bd7c54/test/lp.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/conmax.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-constrained.md) | Linear-programming entry point; constrained minimax routines are separate. Dedicated G2a driver checks three scaled free-variable LPs maximizing -y under x+y>=a, x-y<=b. Known intersections, primal feasibility, solver-reported objective and a nonnegative analytical dual certificate are checked. Infeasibility, unboundedness and general LP robustness are not tested. |
| conmin: `conmin_module` / `conmin_class%solve` | [G2h3b1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G2h3b1b) | configured objective/constraint gradients or internal differences | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/conmin/blob/201f3b29f023baf7bef13ad8f49b906ffb1c7757/src/conmin.F90#L46) · [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/conmin/blob/201f3b29f023baf7bef13ad8f49b906ffb1c7757/src/conmin.F90#L160) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/conmin/blob/201f3b29f023baf7bef13ad8f49b906ffb1c7757/test/TP37.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/conmin.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-directions-fitting.md) | Feasible-directions constrained optimization; analytic-gradient configuration only for this detailed class. Detailed analytic classification requires NFDG=1; NFDG=0 estimates all gradients. |
| fmin: `fmin_module` / `fmin` | [G1a1a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G1a1a) | none | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/fmin/blob/3d094fba0e9b98d132414f8442c7c76d32da1d30/src/fmin_module.f90#L94) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/fmin/blob/3d094fba0e9b98d132414f8442c7c76d32da1d30/test/test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fmin.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-g1.md) | Search interval brackets a scalar minimizer; this is not a general constraint solver. |
| forsolver: `forsolver` / `nlsolver%solve` | [F1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/F1b) | analytic, finite-difference, or complex-step derivative by method | [Source](https://github.com/gha3mi/forsolver/blob/6c0644b287cb49a7310d883415b9b9ba581d51c5/src/forsolver.f90#L15) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/gha3mi/forsolver/blob/6c0644b287cb49a7310d883415b9b9ba581d51c5/test/test_solver3.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/forsolver.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-jacobian-roots.md) | Reviewed scalar Newton configurations; linear and vector-system APIs are separate. |
| fortimize: `ForTimize` / `minimize with GradientDescent` | [G1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G1b) | user gradient | [Source](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/src/minimize.f90#L7) · [Source](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/src/with_derivatives/gradient_descent.f90#L23) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/example/gradient.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortimize.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-gradient-swarm.md) | Fixed-iteration gradient descent with a user gradient. The dedicated independent driver checks 240 SPD-quadratic trajectories against spectral powers and exact callback counts. At this pin the returned objective belongs to the iterate before the final position update; the driver explicitly verifies this and recomputes the endpoint objective. convergence_tolerance is not used by this fixed-count implementation. No adaptive stopping or nonquadratic convergence claim; upstream licensing remains unspecified. |
| fortimize: `ForTimize` / `minimize with NelderMead` | [G1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G1b) | none | [Source](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/src/minimize.f90#L7) · [Source](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/src/gradient_free/nelder_mead.f90#L9) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/fedebenelli/ForTimize/blob/3431b656cd86f89135d0f35bf6afdbd787d9daf3/example/gradient.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortimize.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-optimization-spacing.md) | The existing catalog quadratic program is an independently written API example. Upstream licensing is unspecified; no upstream example code is copied here. Dedicated G1b consumer checks twelve derivative-free solves: coupled positive-definite quadratics in 2/3/5 variables and the two-variable Rosenbrock problem, each from three starts. Known unique minimizers and zero objective supply independent acceptance criteria, and the returned objective is recomputed. Uses the actual kcount evaluation limit; optimizer termination status is not exposed by this wrapper. No upstream example code is copied. Other methods, nonsmooth/global optimization, scaling extremes and concurrent calls are not verified. |
| lbfgsb: `lbfgsb_module` / `setulb` | [G2h1a2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G2h1a2) | user gradient via reverse communication | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/lbfgsb/blob/ce1a9322016d6f31b652393e000a914b63af6fef/src/lbfgsb.f90#L88) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/lbfgsb/blob/ce1a9322016d6f31b652393e000a914b63af6fef/test/driver1.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/lbfgsb.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-constrained.md) | Smooth bound-constrained minimization with limited-memory BFGS. |
| libdogleg-f: `dogleg` / `dl_optimize (dl_optimize_dense2)` | [G1b1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G1b1b) | user residual Jacobian | [Source](https://github.com/ivan-pi/libdogleg-f/blob/32b65ae4f880a052768c05d24c24b79920710681/src/dogleg.f90#L142) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/ivan-pi/libdogleg-f/blob/32b65ae4f880a052768c05d24c24b79920710681/example/lmder_example.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/libdogleg-f.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-g1.md) | Dense nonlinear least squares; Jacobian layout is parameters by residuals, transposed relative to MINPACK. For smooth nonlinear least squares, the user residual Jacobian supplies first-derivative information for the squared-residual objective; this is not a general scalar-objective gradient interface. |
| minpack: `minpack_module` / `hybrj1` | [F2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/F2) | user-supplied analytic Jacobian | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/minpack/blob/c0b5aea9fcd2b83865af921a7a7e881904f8d3c2/src/minpack.f90#L1401) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/minpack/blob/c0b5aea9fcd2b83865af921a7a7e881904f8d3c2/test/test_hybrj.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/minpack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-integrals-tensors-ten.md) | 60 REAL64 triangular nonlinear systems, dimensions 2..6, three alternating-sign, component-scaled patterns and four initial displacement scales. f_i=u_i+u_i**3/8+u_(i-1)**2/4 (last term omitted for i=1), u_i=x_i-i/4. Strict monotonicity in each u_i gives the unique known real root by forward induction. Analytic bidiagonal Jacobian, ier/info=1, both callback paths, returned/recomputed residual consistency and root error <=1e-8. Singular systems and global-convergence claims excluded. |
| minpack: `minpack_module` / `hybrd1` | [F2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/F2) | finite-difference Jacobian | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/minpack/blob/c0b5aea9fcd2b83865af921a7a7e881904f8d3c2/src/minpack.f90#L959) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/minpack/blob/c0b5aea9fcd2b83865af921a7a7e881904f8d3c2/examples/example_hybrd1.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/minpack.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-systems.md) | Powell hybrid solver for a square system of nonlinear equations. The checked F2 driver solves [1-x,10*(y-x**2)]=0 from [-1.2,1] and [2,2], requiring the known root [1,1], small recomputed residuals and documented successful termination. Configuration: finite-difference Jacobian. Other problem dimensions, singular systems and global convergence are not verified. |
