For smooth nonlinear least squares, the user residual Jacobian supplies first-derivative information for the squared-residual objective; this is not a general scalar-objective gradient interface. |

| qnstop: `QNSTOPS_MOD` / `QNSTOPS` | [G2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/vtopt/qnstop/blob/39e8273247f586fca8305ef3d8e879b27b6512d0/src/qnstops.f95#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/vtopt/qnstop/blob/39e8273247f586fca8305ef3d8e879b27b6512d0/src/sample_main_s.f95) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/qnstop.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-matrix-roots-box.md) | Serial QNSTOP for bound-constrained stochastic and global optimization. An FPM wrapper selects unmodified REAL_PRECISION, ZIGARRAY, QNSTOPS and bundled BLAS/LAPACK sources. Explicit CPP suffix handling lets FPM discover .f95 files. Legacy implicit typing/interfaces and argument-mismatch compatibility retain the upstream calling conventions. The seeded two-variable quadratic example checks normal stopping status, bounds, objective value and distance from its known minimizer. It is an accuracy smoke test, not a general global-optimum guarantee. The parallel QNSTOPP driver, noisy objectives, all stopping rules and performance are not validated. Dedicated G2 consumer checks four seeded diagonal-quadratic box problems with known interior minimizers in two/three variables. Verifies normal stopping status, feasibility, finite output, evaluation count, returned-objective consistency and unchanged coordinate/objective tolerances. Active-bound probes failed the position tolerance at both 500 and 2000 iterations; source and actual results are retained under docs/gams-candidates/qnstop. Those face probes and an unreached corner fixture are not counted as verified. Seeds use the pinned GNU intrinsic RNG; this is a deterministic accuracy smoke check, not a general global guarantee. Noisy objectives, parallel variants and all stopping/input modes are not verified. |

| vtmop: `VTMOP_LIB` / `VTMOP_SOLVE` | [G2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/G2) | None required; derivative-free black-box objectives. | [Source](https://github.com/vtopt/VTMOP/blob/794a1de23801731ef6edd1924b52d69b679af6a0/src/vtmop.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/vtopt/VTMOP/blob/794a1de23801731ef6edd1924b52d69b679af6a0/src/vtmop_func.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/vtopt/VTMOP/blob/794a1de23801731ef6edd1924b52d69b679af6a0/src/samples.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/vtmop.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-pareto-diis.md) | Serial derivative-free, bound-constrained multiobjective optimization. An FPM wrapper selects the unchanged solver, surrogate and test-function sources with bundled SLATEC support and links system LP64 BLAS/LAPACK rather than recompiling those bundled libraries. OpenMP linking is enabled, but the checked run uses the serial driver and one execution thread; heap trampolines support internal callbacks in the sandbox. Adapted from the author serial driver and CONVEX function, the seeded 3-variable/2-objective example uses a small evaluation budget, checks feasible design bounds, recomputes both quadratic objectives independently and verifies that no returned point dominates another. It requires multiple distinct trade-off points. This checks the finite returned set, not convergence to the exact global Pareto front or comparative performance. Parallel evaluation, checkpoint recovery, every surrogate and other problem classes are not validated. Dedicated G2 driver checks two squared-distance objectives against an exact Pareto segment in two/three dimensions, with two intrinsic-seed settings per dimension. Every archive point has independently recomputed objectives and projection identities; each run has at least five points within relative distance 0.05 and their segment span exceeds 0.25. The full archive is not uniformly near the Pareto set: the failed all-point 0.15-distance assertion is retained in docs/gams-candidates/vtmop-archive-distance. Both seed settings produced the same observed archive per dimension; they are not evidence of independent stochastic search. |

| fastconv: `conv1d_m` / `conv1d_t%apply` | [J2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/gronki/fastconv/blob/3aaa2432a291df07a10fc5dade0dcd66d7a06dd0/src/conv1d/conv1d.f90#L139) · [Source](https://github.com/gronki/fastconv/blob/3aaa2432a291df07a10fc5dade0dcd66d7a06dd0/src/conv1d/pad.F90#L36) · [Source](https://github.com/gronki/fastconv/blob/3aaa2432a291df07a10fc5dade0dcd66d7a06dd0/src/conv1d/ref.F90#L41) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/gronki/fastconv/blob/3aaa2432a291df07a10fc5dade0dcd66d7a06dd0/README.md) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fastconv.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-convolution.md) | One-dimensional discrete convolution with a supplied kernel. The checked J2 driver uses native REAL32 and preserve_shape=.false. (valid convolution), with a REAL64 direct-sum reference of the rounded inputs. The shape-preserving mode is excluded: at this pin apply allocates output without initialization and the pad/ref paths assign only its interior, leaving boundary entries undefined. |
| spectrum: `spectrum` / `convolve` | [J2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J2) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jchristopherson/spectrum/blob/5af0d5a85eccf971aa6e0fc72b1c7439c3cef536/src/spectrum_convolve.f90#L41) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jchristopherson/spectrum/blob/5af0d5a85eccf971aa6e0fc72b1c7439c3cef536/examples/gaussian_filter_example.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/spectrum.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-convolution.md) | Discrete convolution; filtering and spectral estimation APIs remain broader package-level assignments. The checked J2 driver compares full and central results, including swapped inputs, with independent direct convolution for two length-37 signals and eight kernel lengths. It checks the actual central crop and longer-input output length at this pin. |
| spectrum: `spectrum` / `rfft, irfft` | [J1a1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/J1a1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jchristopherson/spectrum/blob/5af0d5a85eccf971aa6e0fc72b1c7439c3cef536/src/spectrum_fft.f90#L48) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jchristopherson/spectrum/blob/5af0d5a85eccf971aa6e0fc72b1c7439c3cef536/test/spectrum_fft_tests.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/spectrum.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-fourier-geometry.md) | One-dimensional real-input Fourier transform and inverse API. Checked driver covers rfft only: 42 forward transforms against direct trigonometric sums and Parseval energy, including odd/even lengths, zero padding and truncation. irfft is not verified; its source infers original length from half-spectrum length parity, which cannot encode both original parities. |

## Special functions

| Package / public API | GAMS | Derivatives or callback requirements | Pinned evidence and author examples/tests | Qualifications |

| amosf90: `amos` / `f90_zbesj / f90_zbesi / f90_zbesk` | [C10](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C10) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/Euler-37/amosf90/blob/b54a4d4a1d8f7c97acd180ed5bbaae8d089a9fa7/src/amos.f90#L4548) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/Euler-37/amosf90/blob/b54a4d4a1d8f7c97acd180ed5bbaae8d089a9fa7/test/check.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/amosf90.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-special-functions.md) | AMOS module interfaces for complex-argument Bessel functions with noninteger nonnegative order, including modified Bessel functions. The checked main compares J_0.3 and I_0.3 at 1+0.5i with independent convergent power series and K_0.5 with its closed form. Other argument regions, orders, scaling modes and Airy/Hankel routines are not validated. The upstream README warns of possible bugs. Dedicated C10 driver checks 39 complex values: J/I sequences of length two for three noninteger starting orders at three moderate right-half-plane arguments, using independent convergent power series with intrinsic Gamma, and K(1/2) using its closed form. All status/underflow flags must be zero. Scaled mode, branch-cut behavior, extreme arguments and the full library are not verified. Pinned failure: J_0.3(2-0.75i) has scaled error about 0.556 against both the independent series and an 80-digit mpmath reference. Failing source and output are retained under docs/gams-candidates/amosf90. The checked driver uses upper-right-quadrant arguments and excludes this case; no source patch or tolerance relaxation is applied. |
| carlson-elliptic-integrals: `carlson_elliptic_module` / `drf` | [C14](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C14) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/carlson-elliptic-integrals/blob/5f25a59a555b7029505aac71a8341d5851f8abed/src/carlson_elliptic_module.F90#L580) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/carlson-elliptic-integrals.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-special-functions.md) | Carlson symmetric RF evaluated for pendulum periods and checked against independent composite Simpson integration. Dedicated C14 driver checks ten RF values against diagonal identities and two independent Simpson integral forms, plus permutation and homogeneity identities. Positive triples use 2048/4096 panels; four complete elliptic-integral moduli use 4096 panels. Singular/extreme arguments and other Carlson functions are outside the driver. |
| fortran-bessels: `bessels` / `besseli0 / besseli1 / besselk0 / besselk1` | [C10b1](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C10b1) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/perazz/fortran-bessels/blob/7bf8d6b144b00d4201a782ca65f6ae14a84d49a1/src/bessels.f90#L752) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/perazz/fortran-bessels/blob/7bf8d6b144b00d4201a782ca65f6ae14a84d49a1/test/bessels_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortran-bessels.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-special-functions.md) | Real positive arguments and fixed orders zero and one only. Independent values at x=1 and a modified-Bessel Wronskian identity are checked. Upstream documents limitations in other variable-order paths. |
| rpn-calculator-fortran: `funcs / hyper` / `cuberoot / sinc / sech` | [C2](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C2), [C4](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C4) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/src/funcs.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/src/hyper.f90#L1) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/test/test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rpn-calculator-fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-circuits-beta.md) | Numerical routines from the RPN calculator, called directly without its interactive interface. The checked main verifies a negative cube root, sinc and sech at zero, beta(2,3), modified Bessel I0 at zero, and integer gcd/lcm against known results. A checksum-guarded manifest correction sets source-form=default so .f and .f90 files use their respective forms. No numerical sources are changed. The tests do not establish accuracy over full argument ranges or validate the complete calculator. Dedicated C4 consumer verifies real sinc against a 64-term independent series and sech against a stable exponential formula for 133 real arguments, including zero and signed near-zero values. Finite values, symmetry, range and scalar/array agreement are checked. This driver does not verify the separate C2 cube-root mapping or complex overloads. |
| rpn-calculator-fortran: `rat` / `gcd / lcm` | [B](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/B) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/src/rat.f90#L1) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rpn-calculator-fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-number-theory.md) | Numerical routines from the RPN calculator, called directly without its interactive interface. The checked main verifies a negative cube root, sinc and sech at zero, beta(2,3), modified Bessel I0 at zero, and integer gcd/lcm against known results. A checksum-guarded manifest correction sets source-form=default so .f and .f90 files use their respective forms. No numerical sources are changed. The tests do not establish accuracy over full argument ranges or validate the complete calculator. Dedicated GAMS B driver: GCD and LCM checked over nonnegative operands 0..64 against independent divisor/multiple searches. LCM(0,0) divides by zero at this pin and is excluded; signed conventions and overflow are not verified. |
| rpn-calculator-fortran: `fgamma / i0` / `beta / besi0` | [C7](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C7), [C10](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C10) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/src/specfun/gamma.f90#L1) · [Source](https://github.com/scivision/rpn-calculator-fortran/blob/7708e4949f17511d81f4e7dd0271a500f0bc3631/src/specfun/i0.f#L1) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/rpn-calculator-fortran.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-circuits-beta.md) | Numerical routines from the RPN calculator, called directly without its interactive interface. The checked main verifies a negative cube root, sinc and sech at zero, beta(2,3), modified Bessel I0 at zero, and integer gcd/lcm against known results. A checksum-guarded manifest correction sets source-form=default so .f and .f90 files use their respective forms. No numerical sources are changed. The tests do not establish accuracy over full argument ranges or validate the complete calculator. Dedicated C7 consumer verifies 128 positive integer/half-integer beta pairs with independent factorial identities, plus symmetry and both one-step recurrences (512 evaluations). Finite positive values and relative error within 2e-13 are required. C10 Bessel functions, complex beta, poles, large arguments and overflow handling are outside this driver. |
| specfun: `specfun_module` / `bernob` | [C19](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C19) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/jacobwilliams/specfun/blob/e74d9c810d5c9248b5d8ffab4b766e7bb22ffd23/src/specfun.F90#L776) · No matching author example recorded · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/specfun.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-special-functions.md) | Bernoulli numbers; do not classify this procedure as gamma or Bessel just because the package also contains those functions. Dedicated C19 driver checks B0, B1 and even B2..B12 against explicit rational values and 24 integer power sums against direct int64 summation. Pinned bernob leaves odd output positions above B1 unassigned; these positions are never read and their mathematical zeros are supplied locally only for the power-sum application. Small n<2 is also outside the driver because the implementation writes B0..B2 unconditionally. |

| specfunc-fullerton: `specfunc_fullerton` / `integral_si, integral_ci` | [C6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C6) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/src/fullerton_ei.f90#L378) · [Source](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/src/fullerton_ei.f90#L440) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/test/ei/test_ei.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/specfunc-fullerton.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-fullerton-functions-ten.md) | 18 default REAL32 sine/cosine integral values at nine positive dyadic arguments 1/256..16, spanning small-argument, series-transition and oscillatory ranges. Independent high-precision Si/Ci definitions; nonpositive Ci, complex branches and extreme arguments excluded. |
| specfunc-fullerton: `specfunc_fullerton` / `gamma_tricomi` | [C7e](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C7e) | not required | [Source](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/src/fullerton_gamma.f90#L412) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/test/gamma/test_gamma.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/specfunc-fullerton.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-gamma-relative-ten.md) | 49 scaled Tricomi incomplete gamma values for positive shapes 1/2..8 and x=0..8. Independent x**(-a)*lower_gamma(a,x)/Gamma(a) references, with limit 1/Gamma(a+1) at zero. Nonpositive-shape analytic continuation excluded. |
| specfunc-fullerton: `specfunc_fullerton` / `inc_gamma_compl` | [C7e](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C7e) | not required | [Source](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/src/fullerton_gamma.f90#L282) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/test/gamma/test_gamma.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/specfunc-fullerton.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-gamma-relative-ten.md) | 66 unnormalized upper incomplete gamma integrals for strictly positive x=1/8..8, with seven positive shapes 1/2..8 and shapes -2,-1,-1/2,0. Independent high precision tail integrals. The documented x=0 path returns NaN in a retained failed probe and is expressly excluded, not repaired or verified. Near-pole noninteger negative shapes and extremes excluded. |
| specfunc-fullerton: `specfunc_fullerton` / `inc_gamma_compl` | [C7e](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/C7e) | not required | [Source](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/src/fullerton_gamma.f90#L282) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/arjenmarkus/specfunc-fullerton/blob/498b486b8ca18f0289a2d35c6c5bc67eb10aefe3/test/gamma/test_gamma.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/specfunc-fullerton.f90) | 73 unnormalized upper incomplete gamma integrals: 49 positive-shape cases including x=0, plus 24 strictly positive-x cases with shape -2,-1,-1/2,0. Direct high precision tail-integral references. Undefined nonpositive-shape x=0 and near-pole noninteger shapes excluded. Failed at a=1/2,x=0: NaN instead of sqrt(pi); diagnostic retained. Only a separate strictly positive-x consumer can be promoted. |

| econ-toolchain: `dist_mod` / `markov_steady_state / tauchen` | [M](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/M) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/renatomatz/econ-toolchain/blob/301d1659ba3d34973195985c552ca6fcfe2d9f29/src/dist.f90#L62) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/renatomatz/econ-toolchain/blob/301d1659ba3d34973195985c552ca6fcfe2d9f29/test/markov_test.f90) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/renatomatz/econ-toolchain/blob/301d1659ba3d34973195985c552ca6fcfe2d9f29/test/tauchen_test.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/econ-toolchain.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-resampling-markov.md) | Stochastic-process modeling: deterministic stationary probabilities and AR(1) Markov discretization, not a claim of random sampling. The stationary routine uses column-stochastic matrices, while tauchen returns row-stochastic matrices; the consumer transposes explicitly. Checked driver verifies analytic two-state and rank-one equilibria, normal midpoint-cell probabilities, normalization and stationary residuals; no random sampling claim. |
| fjsample: `FJsample` / `Complement, jackError` | [L4](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L4) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/RJaBi/FJsample/blob/8563d2653d1a9d80a355b2064f4b153559698915/src/jack.f90#L21) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/RJaBi/FJsample/blob/8563d2653d1a9d80a355b2064f4b153559698915/app/main.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fjsample.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-resampling-markov.md) | Jackknife resampling uncertainty estimates; keep broad data-analysis class pending a suitable finer match. Checked driver verifies leave-one-out means, sample-mean and squared-mean uncertainty; the returned bias is full estimate minus average resampled estimate, without the conventional n-1 multiplier. |
| forpca: `forpca` / `tpca%pca (SVD mode)` | [D6](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/D6), [L13b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L13b) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/gha3mi/forpca/blob/d5d87badc4c25c647b3618f22560e712728d8165/src/forpca.f90#L29) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/gha3mi/forpca/blob/d5d87badc4c25c647b3618f22560e712728d8165/test/test1.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/forpca.f90) | SVD-based principal directions and covariance analysis; D6 and L13b remain source-supported. A checked variance probe fails: covariance diag(10,3.5) produces latent [100,12.25] and incorrect explained percentages. The SVD branch squares covariance singular values again. See docs/gams-candidates/forpca/covariance-variances-result.json and docs/gams-sparse-integral.md. The ordinary catalog demo checks direction and an independently formed rank-one reconstruction only; no checked PCA variance driver is published. |
| fortsa: `fortsa_stats_m` / `acvf, acvf2acf` | [L10a2a](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L10a2a) | not applicable | [Source](https://github.com/zoziha/fortsa/blob/d31da12286617ab82e06e9fa0a6f20d11778581c/src/fortsa_stats.f90#L18) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/zoziha/fortsa/blob/d31da12286617ab82e06e9fa0a6f20d11778581c/test/stats/test_stats.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fortsa.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-applied-methods.md) | Sample autocovariances and autocorrelations of a single time series; ARIMA fitting is a different API. Dedicated L10a2a driver checks 12 direct/FFT autocovariance calls and 9 normalization calls on base, shifted, scaled and constant 8-point series, at all lags 0..7, against independent centered scalar lag sums divided by N. Zero-variance correlation is not requested; missing data, ARIMA and other estimators are outside the driver. |
| fstats: `fstats_binomial_distribution` / `binomial_distribution%pdf, %cdf, %survival, %log_pdf` | [L5a1b](https://gams.nist.gov/cgi-bin/serve.cgi/Class/L5a1b) | not required | [Source](https://github.com/jchristopherson/fstats/blob/5fdb86d1e7fa057d776d7d9477942fd6d387dd2f/src/distributions/fstats_binomial_distribution.f90#L35) · [Author example/test](https://github.com/jchristopherson/fstats/blob/5fdb86d1e7fa057d776d7d9477942fd6d387dd2f/tests/fstats_distribution_tests.f90) · [Catalog main](../fpm_catalog/demos/fstats.f90) · [Verified driver](gams-fstats-summaries-ten.md) | 165 REAL64 distribution inputs. PDF/PMF, CDF, strict upper survival and finite log-density checked with independent 80/120 digit references. Probability relative tolerance 2e-9 with floor 1e-30; invalid parameters and infinite log values excluded. |

The L13 driver verifies spherical/exponential/Gaussian covariances, 21 two-observation ordinary kriging systems, closed-form weights and variances, constant-field reproduction and reflection symmetry. It separates real32 covariance error from real64 solver residuals, and does not verify the separate E2 mapping. GSLIB source-evidence hashes use UTF-8/LF normalization. |

Other quantity dimensions, parsing, overflow and compile-time rejection of invalid dimensional expressions are not verified. |

## Evidence and next steps

- Source archives were downloaded at catalog revisions and checked against the catalog archive SHA-256 hashes. Procedure and example records include paths, source SHA-256 hashes, and source line anchors.
- Author examples and tests are usage candidates, not a promise that they run unchanged within sandbox limits. The catalog main may exercise only one of the package’s mapped APIs. The separately recorded [optimization drivers](gams-g1.md), [quadrature drivers](gams-quadrature.md), [scalar-root drivers](gams-roots.md), [linear-algebra drivers](gams-linear-algebra.md) [ODE drivers](gams-ode.md), [interpolation drivers](gams-interpolation.md) [transform drivers](gams-transforms.md), [constrained-optimization drivers](gams-constrained.md) [special-function drivers](gams-special-functions.md), [statistics drivers](gams-statistics.md), [random-generator drivers](gams-random.md), [differentiation drivers](gams-differentiation.md), [nonlinear-system drivers](gams-systems.md), [sparse/positive-definite drivers](gams-sparse.md), [cubature/rule drivers](gams-cubature.md), [surface-interpolation drivers](gams-surfaces.md), [stiff-ODE drivers](gams-stiff.md), [polynomial-root drivers](gams-polynomial.md), [convolution drivers](gams-convolution.md), [regression drivers](gams-regression.md) and [matrix-equation drivers](gams-matrix-equations.md) carry the driver-verification designation.
- Generic APIs retain broader classes where real/complex arithmetic, matrix structure, derivatives, or constraints depend on configuration. Detailed optimization mappings state the derivative mode; derivative-free modes need separate records.
- Next, refine the discovery-only queue, split overloaded APIs where useful, and adapt suitable author examples into independently checked drivers. Use common mathematical problems when comparing packages; one successful example is not a performance ranking.

## Maintenance

Edit procedure mappings in [gams.json](../fpm_catalog/gams.json) and update the coverage ledger [gams-numerical-review.json](../fpm_catalog/gams-numerical-review.json). Revision and archive pins must match the selected catalog. The ledger explicitly records supporting utilities and prevents a newly added numerical package from silently escaping review.

Run `python xpackage_gams.py` to validate and regenerate the review pages, `python xfpm_examples.py` to publish search metadata, and `python xcatalog_export.py` to export the reusable classifications. These commands do not compile packages or redeploy Modal.
